The root is the sole `CANONICAL` row. Its upstream is `origin/main`; capture-time divergence is `0 behind, 17 ahead`. Therefore a clean production source alone does not make it release-clean: it is explicitly **non-release-clean** until the upstream relationship is settled. The `M .planning/STATE.md` status line is the known orchestrator phase-start tracking diff, not unclassified product work.

## Expansion Command Evidence

- **REF-ENUM-01:** `git for-each-ref --format='%(refname)%00%(objectname)%00%(upstream)%00%(upstream:track)' refs/heads refs/remotes refs/tags` — 155 identities, exit `0`; selected release/archive identities have separate rows above, including equal-OID tag identities.
- **RANGE-CMD-01:** `git rev-list --left-right --count` and `git log --left-right --cherry-mark --oneline` for every archive and attached release/worktree range listed in `RANGE-*`, exit `0`.
- **REL-CMD-01:** `shasum -a 256` plus `git status --short -- <file>` for release target, each tracked publish summary, and Phase 160 summary, exit `0`.
- **FSCK-2026-08-22T15:36:55Z:** `git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-dangling`, exit `0`; counts and one row per unreachable commit are above.
- **NONE contract:** no enumerated category was empty at capture. If a future capture has no stash, relevant ref/range, release proof, or selected object, it must add the corresponding successful `NONE-*` zero-count row; omission never means empty.

## Assessment Recapture and Evidence

**Assessment capture time (UTC):** 2026-08-22T15:57:00Z
**Assessment HEAD:** `305c9b41d62e9e1470770cd057495308340cf91f` (`docs(161-01): complete canonical workspace evidence plan`)

The Plan 01 documentation commit advanced canonical `HEAD` from the immutable first-capture marker. This append-only recapture is therefore required before assessment; it does not revise the original snapshot. The six registered paths, the sole stash selector/OID mapping, every inventoried named-ref OID, and the 1,805-object `fsck --no-reflogs` set remained the same. A future mismatch in any of those inputs aborts this assessment and requires another complete append-only snapshot.

### Content and Unique-Work Evidence

- **EVID-WT-WT-01 … EVID-WT-WT-06:** `git -C '<registered path>' status --short --branch`, `git -C '<registered path>' rev-parse HEAD`, and `git show --stat --format=fuller '<oid>'` all exited `0`. WT-03 remains a dirty detached worktree; its three publish-summary diffs are non-empty and hash to `75ea168315ebff101a2dd060499f86a73f688ca5597837bb22dec1dc5b16ce69`, so it is retained. The other attached branch heads contain release-policy/authorization/recovery content and are handed to Phase 162 instead of inferred disposable.
- **EVID-STASH-CONTENT:** `git stash show --stat --include-untracked 'stash@{0}'` and `git show --stat --format=fuller '024fc1ba0379d6bfb9b466fab407d94a94a2fa5a'` exited `0`; the stash changes `.planning/config.json` and `mailglass-publish-summary.json`. It is commit-backed content, not an empty selector.
- **EVID-REF-GRAPH / EVID-RANGE-GRAPH:** for every named archive, branch, remote, and tag identity, `git show --stat --format=fuller '<oid>'`, `git rev-list --left-right --count 'main...<ref>'`, and `git log --left-right --cherry-mark --oneline 'main...<ref>'` were run with quoted operands. Named archive refs have right-side history; release/recovery refs have either main ancestry or an unresolved remote/release interpretation. Equal-OID release tags remain separate identities and independently hand off that interpretation.
- **EVID-RELEASE-CONTENT:** `shasum -a 256` and the WT-03 binary diff were run for `release-target.json` and each publish summary. The three dirty summaries describe the 2.5.0/2.2.0 package set and include source/file-list changes; they are concrete local release evidence, not stale-name evidence. Their external meaning remains a Phase 162 question.
- **EVID-OBJ-CONTENT-GRAPH:** an iterated quoted `git show --stat --format='%H%x09%s%x09%P' '<oid>'` completed for all **1,805** listed `OBJ-*` commits (1,613 distinct subjects). The sample spans phase-completion documentation, release-policy tests, and product/release work; no object is classified disposable from `fsck` or age. This bulk per-object scan deliberately preserves uncertain objects until a reflog-aware and content-specific future review.

### Reachability Evidence

For every named OID, `git branch --contains '<oid>'`, `git tag --contains '<oid>'`, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor '<oid>' main` were run. Representative results: `chore/authorize-release-91353` and all three `v2.5.0` package tags are ancestors of `main`; `origin/fix/post-publish-consumer-postgres` and `origin/fix/post-publish-host-swoosh` are also contained by `main`; archive refs, detached/recovery branches, and the v2.6 milestone tag are not thereby removable because they retain divergent or historical content. `git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-dangling` still reports exactly 1,805 `OBJ-*` commits; the `--no-reflogs` classification is graph evidence only, never deletion authority.

## Disposition Rationale

Each non-sentinel row now has exactly one permitted disposition, an `EVID-*` citation, a preservation/handoff target, and a non-executing next action. `archive` means keep the named existing archive ref; it does not create a new ref. `handoff` means Phase 162 must answer the concrete remote/release question; it does not authorize merge or removal. `retain` is used wherever dirty, stash, unreachable, or otherwise uncertain content requires preservation. No row is `remove`, because the evidence does not prove absence of unique work with the required reflog-aware confidence.

### Disposition Coverage

| metric | count |
| --- | ---: |
| non-sentinel assessed rows | 1849 |
| retain | 1810 |
| handoff | 27 |
| archive | 12 |
| merge | 0 |
| remove | 0 |
| pending | 0 |

Stable key/order is `category + identity`; the existing category blocks and bytewise identities were retained. The assessment is idempotent at this capture HEAD: it updates existing stable rows, creates no duplicate identities, and performs no branch/ref creation, merge, removal, stash change, reset, force action, garbage collection, or history rewrite.

### Assessment Matrix Validation

The fresh enumerators reconcile to the stable ledger: 6 worktrees, 1 stash, 155 enumerated refs (including the separately represented equal-OID release tags), 10 divergent ranges, 5 local release-proof rows, and 1,805 unreachable-object rows. Their 1,849 non-sentinel item rows have 1,849 `EVID-*` references and 1,849 allowed dispositions. Category/identity duplicate detection returned zero duplicates, and the category blocks retain their capture ordering; shared OIDs only share evidence, never a disposition row.

The permitted next action is deliberately non-executing for all rows in this phase. In particular, no `remove` decision is inferred from age, a detached worktree, absence from `main`, or `fsck --no-reflogs` output. Phase 162 owns the named remote/release handoffs; a later cleanup phase may act only after its stated preservation and graph/content prerequisites are met.
